admin/admin_log.md holds an entry for each Unchecked statement you ran.


Common mistakes

Writing a column with its Database

Write a column's name out as you reach it in Python, and the Hive has three parts in it:

unchecked_hive(f"SELECT ops.jobs.job_id FROM {jobs}")

Hive refuses it, saying it can't find the table or column ops: after FROM ops.jobs AS jobs the table is called jobs. The Example database's own small executor runs it anyway, so try it at work. Drop the column's text in with {jobs.job_id}.

A table's text after DESCRIBE

An f-string gives a table's text with its AS, which only FROM and JOIN take:

to_hive(unchecked_hive(f"DESCRIBE {jobs}"))

Python shows
'DESCRIBE ops.jobs AS jobs'

Hive refuses that. Write the name alone, "DESCRIBE ops.jobs", as the steps do for SHOW PARTITIONS.

Sending a read with run_execute

The Toolbox doesn't read your text to choose a call: it sends it through the one you called. A read sent with run_execute goes to your execute, which gives back nothing, so its rows are lost:

run_execute(unchecked_hive("SHOW PARTITIONS ops.job_events"), execute)

Python prints
execute: SHOW PARTITIONS ops.job_events

28. Upgrade your Project to a new Toolbox version Intermediate

Goal

Carry your Project's files, its Table references, Building blocks, Table notebooks and the rest, to a newer Toolbox you copied in, so you never redo by hand what a Project action made once. A Project is your folder of scripts at work, in the Toolbox's fixed layout; a Project action is a one-time change to its files, such as starting it or adding a Table reference. An Upgrade is the Project action that carries the files from one Toolbox version to the next: it makes each change that needs no judgement itself, and marks each one it leaves for you at its line.

When you'd use it

If you copied in a newer sqlglot_composer download, upgrade the Project: after deleting the old folder, copying in the new one and restarting the kernel, as the README's Update says.
If the Project was made by Toolbox 7.1 or later, project.upgrade() carries it: from 7.1 on, every Toolbox version ships the step that carries a Project to it from the version before.
If you have no Project yet, do Start a Project first, and read Keep the Project in order for project.check().


Steps

1. Start from a Project an older Toolbox wrote

Goal: a Project to upgrade. Work in your own Project. This page makes one: a copy of the Intermediate Example project, with the files and the folder 8.0's Upgrade adds taken out, and 7.2 recorded as the version that last wrote it, so it looks to project.upgrade() like a Project 7.2 left:

import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import sqlglot_composer
download = Path(sqlglot_composer.__file__).parents[1]  # -> Path
_ = shutil.copytree(download / "example_projects" / "intermediate", ".", dirs_exist_ok=True)

It writes admin/project_version.txt too.

admin/project_version.txt says which Toolbox version last wrote a Project; one 7.1 made has none, and is taken to be 7.1.

Done when admin/project_version.txt says 7.2. Then step 2.

2. See that it needs an Upgrade

Goal: know the Project is behind the Toolbox. Importing the Toolbox in the Project warns that an older Toolbox last wrote it, the next time you restart the kernel (this page imported it before writing 7.2 in the version file, so it shows no warning), and project.check() says so too. Nothing is refused meanwhile: your notebooks still run.

from sqlglot_composer import project
print(project.check())

Python prints
The Project was last written by Toolbox 7.2, and this Toolbox is 8.2: run project.upgrade() in a new cell of admin/check_project.ipynb, or of admin/workflows/upgrade/upgrade.ipynb once the Project has one.

Done when project.check() names the version that last wrote the Project. Then step 3.

5. Make each change it marked

Goal: no change left for you. Make each change, delete its mark, and tick its box. Open admin/databases.py, fill in DEV and PROD with your own databases' names, and delete the two # UPGRADE 8.0: lines. This page stands in for your editor:

databases = Path("admin/databases.py")  # -> Path
text = databases.read_text(encoding="utf-8")  # -> str
text = text.replace('"<DEV_DATABASE>"', '"mart_dev"').replace('"<PROD_DATABASE>"', '"mart"')
kept = [line for line in text.splitlines(keepends=True)
        if not line.startswith("# UPGRADE")]  # -> list
_ = databases.write_text("".join(kept), encoding="utf-8")

It writes admin/databases.py

"""databases - the two Databases you own: DEV, where you build and check
tables, and PROD, where tables other people rely on are written. Set each once; the Workflows
read them.
"""

DEV = "mart_dev"  # such as "mart_dev"
PROD = "mart"  # such as "mart"

Done when admin/databases.py names both databases and holds no # UPGRADE line. Then check it worked, below.

Check it worked

project.check() says nothing of the version, and lists no # UPGRADE mark left:

print(project.check())

Python prints
Every Table folder, Table reference and Building block is registered, and each one registered is there.

Run again, the Upgrade does nothing:

print(project.upgrade())

Python prints
The Project is at 8.2 already, as this Toolbox is: nothing to upgrade.

Before you go on, check that:

project.check() names no Toolbox version and no # UPGRADE mark;
project.upgrade() says there is nothing to upgrade;
each box under For you to do in each admin/upgrade_*.md checklist is ticked.


Common mistakes

Pasting the new folder over the old one

Delete the old sqlglot_composer folder first, then copy in the new one: a file a newer version dropped would otherwise be left behind, and the import stops on it, since the folder checks itself against its version.txt, as the README's Update says.

Upgrading a Project made before 7.1

An Upgrade carries a Project from 7.1 on: bring an older one to 7.1 by hand, as CHANGES.md says, then upgrade it.

Leaving the marks

Each # UPGRADE mark is a change only you can make: make it, then delete the mark. project.check() lists every one left.

Next

Keep the Project in order: project.check() and the registry.
Convert someone else's Hive: what admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb is for.


Toolbox names used: project.check, project.upgrade




29. Follow a Workflow Intermediate

Goal

Do a whole job from its Workflow notebook, step by step, with nothing typed twice. A Workflow is an ordered list of steps that takes a Project, your folder of scripts at work, from what you bring to one end goal, such as converting scripts or promoting tables. Each Workflow has a notebook in admin/workflows/, a folder for each, and each Workflow below is drawn and listed from the steps its notebook runs. A Decision is one thing about one table that only you decide, such as its location or its Key, the columns whose values name exactly one row.

When you'd use it

If you start one of the jobs below, follow its Workflow's notebook, as the steps below do.
If you don't know which step of a Workflow comes next, run project.check(): it names the next step of each Workflow you have started.
If you want every step left run for you, run the notebook's last cell, project.run_workflow(...), in place of the steps' cells: it runs every step left, the calls to the warehouse too, up to the first only you can do: a Decision, or a check of what a step printed.
If you haven't sent anything yet, run project.run_workflow(...) with dry_run=True first: it sends nothing through your execute and says the Hive each step would send. It runs for real convert and the references Workflow's first step, which read DESCRIBE, and each step that changes only files up to the first that would send; after that it names each such step, "would run: ...", and runs none.
If you want only the steps that change files run, run project.do_next().
If you have no Project yet, do Start a Project first.


Steps

1. Start a Project

Goal: a Project whose admin/workflows/ folder holds a notebook for each Workflow, each waiting for its names cell:

from sqlglot_composer import project
project.start()

Python shows
Started a Project in the folder you're working in: admin/, references/, building_blocks/,
tables/ and warehouse.py.
Next: set conn, your connection, in warehouse.py.

It writes 22 files

admin/admin_log.md
admin/check_project.ipynb
admin/check_references.ipynb
admin/databases.py
admin/new_block.ipynb
admin/project_version.txt
admin/refresh_reference_sheet.ipynb
admin/registry.csv
admin/remove.ipynb
admin/run_tables.ipynb
admin/sent.csv
admin/templates/building_block.py
admin/templates/quality_checks.py
admin/templates/saved_table.py
admin/templates/saved_table_reference.py
admin/workflows/change_a_saved_table/change_a_saved_table.ipynb
admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb
admin/workflows/new_table/new_table.ipynb
admin/workflows/promote/promote.ipynb
admin/workflows/references/references.ipynb
admin/workflows/upgrade/upgrade.ipynb
warehouse.py

Done when project.start() says it started a Project. Then step 2.

2. Choose the Workflow for your job

Goal: the one Workflow whose end goal is yours. Each Workflow is described after these steps, with its chart, its names cell and its steps.

If you want a Workflow that turns Hive someone else wrote, one query or a batch of scripts, into your own code, choose convert: admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb.
If you want a Workflow that carries Saved tables from your Dev Database to your Prod Database, choose promote: admin/workflows/promote/promote.ipynb.
If you want a Workflow that makes a Table folder for each new Saved table, choose new_table: admin/workflows/new_table/new_table.ipynb.
If you want a Workflow that writes a Table reference for each table your Project reads, from its DESCRIBE, choose references: admin/workflows/references/references.ipynb.
If you want a Workflow that carries the Project's files to the Toolbox you copied in, once you have restarted the kernel, choose upgrade: admin/workflows/upgrade/upgrade.ipynb.
If you want a Workflow that changes a Saved table in the warehouse with one of the admin module's Admin statements, each logged in admin/admin_log.md, choose change_a_saved_table: admin/workflows/change_a_saved_table/change_a_saved_table.ipynb.


In each chart, a plain box is a step that changes only files, or only prints; a box with double edges a call to the warehouse, yours to run; a diamond a decision, yours to make; and a box with round corners something a step needs or makes.

Done when you know the notebook to open. Then step 3.

3. Open its notebook

Goal: the notebook whose cells run your job's steps. Each Workflow notebook starts by finding the Project, then imports what its steps call:

from sqlglot_composer import (
    admin,
    hive_settings,
    project,
    run_execute,
    run_query,
    show_hive,
)
from warehouse import query, execute

Done when its first cells run without an error. Then step 4.

4. Fill in its names cell, and save it

Goal: the start materials the Workflow works on, named once. Write each name its names cell asks for, as its Workflow's section below shows, and save the notebook.

Done when each name the cell asks for is filled in and saved. Then step 5.

5. Run its steps in order

Goal: the Workflow's end goal reached. Run each step's cell in turn, as its Workflow's section below lists them, or its last cell in their place. At a step that is a decision, yours to make, make it: write your answer to each open Decision in the decisions cell, beside the names cell, and save it, or read what the step before printed. Then run the next step.

Done when project.check() says every step of the Workflow is done. Then check it worked, below.

The Workflows, for steps 2 to 5:

The convert Workflow

Turns Hive someone else wrote, one query or a batch of scripts, into your own code: each table the scripts write becomes a Saved table in your Dev Database, with its Table folder, and what only you can decide of it, a Decision, answered in the decisions cell.

Its chart, drawn from the steps of its notebook, admin/workflows/convert/convert.ipynb:

Its chart, as Mermaid text, which GitHub and VS Code draw```mermaid
flowchart TD
    s0[["1. Convert the scripts: each table they write a Saved table in dev"]]:::warehouse_call
    s1{"2. Answer each table's open Decisions in the decisions cell"}:::your_decision
    s2["3. Write each answer into its table's Table reference"]:::files_only
    s3[["4. Create each table in dev"]]:::warehouse_call
    s4[["5. Seed each table that reads itself, once, from its original"]]:::warehouse_call
    s5[["6. Write each table in dev"]]:::warehouse_call
    s6[["7. Compare each table with its original"]]:::warehouse_call
    t0("Hive scripts"):::thing
    t1("your Dev Database's name"):::thing
    t2("read access to the sources"):::thing
    t3("a dev Table folder per table the scripts write"):::thing
    t4("a Table reference per table"):::thing
    t5("each answer in the decisions cell"):::thing
    t6("each answer written in"):::thing
    t7("each made in dev"):::thing
    t8("each seeded"):::thing
    t9("each written in dev"):::thing
    t10("each compared with its original"):::thing
    t0 --> s0
    t1 --> s0
    t2 --> s0
    s0 --> t3
    s0 --> t4
    t4 --> s1
    s1 --> t5
    t5 --> s2
    s2 --> t6
    t6 --> s3
    s3 --> t7
    t7 --> s4
    s4 --> t8
    t8 --> s5
    s5 --> t9
    t9 --> s6
    s6 --> t10
    classDef files_only fill:#e3f2e1,stroke:#4a8a3f
    classDef warehouse_call fill:#e1ecf7,stroke:#3f6a9a
    classDef your_decision fill:#fbf1d6,stroke:#a8842a
    classDef thing fill:#ffffff,stroke:#888888
```

Its names cell, which you fill in and save first:

# the batch's name, plain lowercase, such as "weekly" (or, for the last cell alone,
# a list of batches converted already)
NAME = ''
# the scripts' files, from this folder, such as ["daily.hql"], or [] to read HIVE
FILES = []
# the Hive as text, such as """SELECT ...""", or None to read FILES
HIVE = None
# the database of a table the Hive names without one
DATABASE = 'default'
# a name of your own for a table's copy in dev, by the table's full name, such as
# {"mart.team_fails": "team_fails"} for mart_dev.team_fails: left out, a copy is
# <table>_<its database>, such as mart_dev.team_fails_mart
RENAME = {}
# each staging table convert names, kept or folded, such as {"ops_reports.failed":
# "keep"}: left out, a temporary or DROPped one is folded, any other kept
STAGING = {}

Its decisions cell, beside it, where you answer each open Decision:

DECISIONS = {}  # your answer to each open Decision, by the table's name as
# project.check() writes it, such as
# {"mart_dev.weekly_ops": {"location": "/data/team/mart_dev.db/weekly",
#                          "key": ["job_id"], "types": {"fails": "bigint"}}}
# "key": None says no columns name one row. project.check() names each one open;
# a Key proposed from a query is made, but an answer here changes it

Its steps, in order:

Convert the scripts: each table they write a Saved table in dev (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


project.convert(NAME, HIVE, files=FILES, query=query, database=DATABASE,
                rename=RENAME, staging=STAGING)

Answer each table's open Decisions in the decisions cell (it is a decision, yours to make).
Write each answer into its table's Table reference (it changes only files, so project.do_next() runs it for you).


project.write_decisions(DECISIONS)

Create each table in dev (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


for table in project.batch_tables(NAME):
    run_execute(project.load(table).create(), execute)

Seed each table that reads itself, once, from its original (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


run_execute(hive_settings("dynamic_partitions"), execute)
for table in project.batch_tables(NAME, seeded=True):
    run_execute(project.load(table).seed(), execute)

Write each table in dev (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


project.run_tables(tables=project.batch_tables(NAME), execute=execute)

Compare each table with its original (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


project.compare_batch(NAME, query=query)

Its last cell runs every step left, in place of their cells:

# Every step left, in place of their cells, up to the first only you can do.
# Add dry_run=True to see the Hive it would send, sending nothing through your
# execute: help(project.run_workflow) says the steps it still runs:
project.run_workflow("convert", query=query, execute=execute)

Its names cell, which you fill in and save first:

# the dev tables, by their names in your Dev Database, such as

The new_table Workflow

Makes a Table folder for each new Saved table: from its query, when QUERIES gives one, its logic written for you; else from a Template, its logic for you to write. A Building block, a piece several notebooks share, is made with project.new_block(...).

Its chart, drawn from the steps of its notebook, admin/workflows/new_table/new_table.ipynb:

Its chart, as Mermaid text, which GitHub and VS Code draw```mermaid
flowchart TD
    s0["1. Make each Table folder, converting each table's query QUERIES gives"]:::files_only

    s3{"4. Answer each table's open Decisions in the decisions cell"}:::your_decision
    s4["5. Write each answer into its table's Table reference"]:::files_only
    s5[["6. Check each table's rows with preview(), which writes nothing"]]:::warehouse_call
    s6[["7. Create and write each in dev"]]:::warehouse_call
    s7[["8. Compare each table made from a query with its query"]]:::warehouse_call
    t0("the tables' names"):::thing
    t1("a Table folder"):::thing
    t2("what each table should hold"):::thing
    t3("its logic"):::thing
    t4("its Table reference"):::thing
    t5("each answer in the decisions cell"):::thing
    t6("a dev Table folder whose logic loads"):::thing
    t7("its rows, checked"):::thing
    t8("written in dev"):::thing
    t9("each from a query compared with it"):::thing
    t0 --> s0
    s0 --> t1
    t1 --> s1
    t2 --> s1
    s1 --> t3
    t3 --> s2

    t4 --> s3

    s4 --> t6

    s5 --> t7

    t4 --> s6
    s6 --> t8
    t8 --> s7
    s7 --> t9
    classDef files_only fill:#e3f2e1,stroke:#4a8a3f
    classDef warehouse_call fill:#e1ecf7,stroke:#3f6a9a
    classDef your_decision fill:#fbf1d6,stroke:#a8842a
    classDef thing fill:#ffffff,stroke:#888888
```

Its names cell, which you fill in and save first:

# the new tables, by their names in the warehouse, such as
# ["mart_dev.daily_job_runs"]
TABLES = []
# each table's query, by its name without its database, as Hive text or a .hql file
# in admin/workflows/new_table/, such as {"daily_job_runs": "daily_job_runs.hql"}
# for mart_dev.daily_job_runs: it is made in your Dev Database, its logic written
# from its query
QUERIES = {}

Its decisions cell, beside it, where you answer each open Decision:

DECISIONS = {}  # your answer to each open Decision, by the table's name as
# project.check() writes it, such as
# {"mart_dev.weekly_ops": {"location": "/data/team/mart_dev.db/weekly",
#                          "key": ["job_id"], "types": {"fails": "bigint"}}}
# "key": None says no columns name one row. project.check() names each one open;
# a Key proposed from a query is made, but an answer here changes it

Its steps, in order:

Make each Table folder, converting each table's query QUERIES gives (it changes only files, so project.do_next() runs it for you).


project.new_table(TABLES, queries=QUERIES)

Write each table's logic in its Table notebook, and save it (it is a decision, yours to make).


for table in TABLES:
    project.load(table).preview()  # says what stops it, if anything does

Write each Table reference from its logic (it changes only files, so project.do_next() runs it for you).


project.reference_from_logic(TABLES)

Answer each table's open Decisions in the decisions cell (it is a decision, yours to make).
Write each answer into its table's Table reference (it changes only files, so project.do_next() runs it for you).


project.write_decisions(DECISIONS)

Check each table's rows with preview(), which writes nothing (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


for table in TABLES:
    print(run_query(project.load(table).preview(), query))

Create and write each in dev (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


for table in TABLES:
    run_execute(project.load(table).create(), execute)
project.run_tables(tables=TABLES, execute=execute)

Compare each table made from a query with its query (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


for name in QUERIES:
    print(project.compare_batch(name, query=query))

Its last cell runs every step left, in place of their cells:

# Every step left, in place of their cells, up to the first only you can do.
# Add dry_run=True to see the Hive it would send, sending nothing through your
# execute: help(project.run_workflow) says the steps it still runs:
project.run_workflow("new_table", query=query, execute=execute)

The references Workflow

Writes a Table reference for each table your Project reads, from its DESCRIBE.

Its chart, drawn from the steps of its notebook, admin/workflows/references/references.ipynb:

Its chart, as Mermaid text, which GitHub and VS Code draw```mermaid
flowchart TD
    s0[["1. Write the Table references from DESCRIBE"]]:::warehouse_call
    s1{"2. Answer each table's open Decisions in the decisions cell"}:::your_decision
    s2["3. Write each answer into its table's Table reference"]:::files_only
    s3["4. Refresh the Reference sheet and the Database modules"]:::files_only
    t0("table names your query can DESCRIBE"):::thing
    t1("Table references with TODO lines"):::thing
    t2("each answer in the decisions cell"):::thing
    t3("filled-in Table references"):::thing
    t4("the Reference sheet"):::thing
    t5("the Database modules"):::thing
    t0 --> s0
    s0 --> t1
    t1 --> s1
    s1 --> t2
    t2 --> s2
    s2 --> t3
    t3 --> s3
    s3 --> t4
    s3 --> t5
    classDef files_only fill:#e3f2e1,stroke:#4a8a3f
    classDef warehouse_call fill:#e1ecf7,stroke:#3f6a9a
    classDef your_decision fill:#fbf1d6,stroke:#a8842a
    classDef thing fill:#ffffff,stroke:#888888
```

Its names cell, which you fill in and save first:

# the tables, by their names in the warehouse, such as ["mart.daily_job_runs"]
TABLES = []

Its decisions cell, beside it, where you answer each open Decision:

DECISIONS = {}  # your answer to each open Decision, by the table's name as
# project.check() writes it, such as
# {"mart_dev.weekly_ops": {"location": "/data/team/mart_dev.db/weekly",
#                          "key": ["job_id"], "types": {"fails": "bigint"}}}
# "key": None says no columns name one row. project.check() names each one open;
# a Key proposed from a query is made, but an answer here changes it

Its steps, in order:

Write the Table references from DESCRIBE (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


project.add_references(TABLES, query=query)

Answer each table's open Decisions in the decisions cell (it is a decision, yours to make).
Write each answer into its table's Table reference (it changes only files, so project.do_next() runs it for you).


project.write_decisions(DECISIONS)

Refresh the Reference sheet and the Database modules (it changes only files, so project.do_next() runs it for you).


project.refresh_reference_sheet(TABLES)

Its last cell runs every step left, in place of their cells:

# Every step left, in place of their cells, up to the first only you can do.
# Add dry_run=True to see the Hive it would send, sending nothing through your
# execute: help(project.run_workflow) says the steps it still runs:
project.run_workflow("references", query=query, execute=execute)

    t1("the new Toolbox copied in"):::thing
    t2("the Project at the new version"):::thing
    t3("UPGRADE marks"):::thing
    t4("every UPGRADE mark dealt with"):::thing
    t0 --> s0
    t1 --> s0
    s0 --> t2
    s0 --> t3
    t3 --> s1
    s1 --> t4
    classDef files_only fill:#e3f2e1,stroke:#4a8a3f
    classDef your_decision fill:#fbf1d6,stroke:#a8842a
    classDef thing fill:#ffffff,stroke:#888888
```

Its steps, in order:

Upgrade the Project's files (it changes only files, or only prints: run its cell).


project.upgrade()

Make each change an UPGRADE mark asks for, and delete the mark (it is a decision, yours to make).


Its last cell runs every step left, in place of their cells:

# Every step left, in place of their cells, up to the first only you can do.
# Add dry_run=True to see the Hive it would send, sending nothing through your
# execute: help(project.run_workflow) says the steps it still runs:
project.run_workflow("upgrade")

The change_a_saved_table Workflow

Changes a Saved table in the warehouse with one of the admin module's Admin statements, each logged in admin/admin_log.md.

Its chart, drawn from the steps of its notebook, admin/workflows/change_a_saved_table/change_a_saved_table.ipynb:

Its chart, as Mermaid text, which GitHub and VS Code draw```mermaid
flowchart TD
    s0{"1. Write the Admin statement for the change"}:::your_decision
    s1["2. Print its Hive, and run the check its Check first line names"]:::files_only
    s2[["3. Send it, then any check after it, such as admin.check_the_copy"]]:::warehouse_call
    t0("a Saved table"):::thing
    t1("the change wanted"):::thing
    t2("an Admin statement"):::thing
    t3("its Hive, read and checked"):::thing
    t4("the change sent"):::thing
    t5("the change logged"):::thing
    t0 --> s0
    t1 --> s0
    s0 --> t2
    t2 --> s1
    s1 --> t3
    t3 --> s2
    s2 --> t4
    s2 --> t5
    classDef files_only fill:#e3f2e1,stroke:#4a8a3f
    classDef warehouse_call fill:#e1ecf7,stroke:#3f6a9a
    classDef your_decision fill:#fbf1d6,stroke:#a8842a
    classDef thing fill:#ffffff,stroke:#888888
```

Its names cell, which you fill in and save first:

# the Saved table to change, such as "mart.daily_job_runs"
TABLE = ''

Its steps, in order:

Write the Admin statement for the change (it is a decision, yours to make).


from sqlglot_composer import between, equals  # where= takes a Condition
table = project.table_reference(TABLE)
# such as where=equals(table.dt, "2026-09-24"), or a range of days,
# where=between(table.dt, "2026-09-01", "2026-09-30"), dt its partition column
change = admin.drop_partitions(table, where=...)  # or another: help(admin)

Print its Hive, and run the check its Check first line names (it changes only files, or only prints: run its cell).


show_hive(change)

Send it, then any check after it, such as admin.check_the_copy (it is a call to the warehouse, yours to run).


run_execute(change, execute)

Its last cell runs every step left, in place of their cells:

# Every step left, in place of their cells, up to the first only you can do.
# Add dry_run=True to see the Hive it would send, sending nothing through your
# execute: help(project.run_workflow) says the steps it still runs:
project.run_workflow("change_a_saved_table", query=query, execute=execute, change=change)

Check it worked

Before you go on, check that:

project.check() names the next step of each Workflow you have started, or says that every step is done;
project.check() names no open Decision for the tables of the Workflow you ran.


Common mistakes

A step named again after you ran it

project.check() names a step no file tells of, such as change_a_saved_table's "Print its Hive", until a later step is done, and says so: once you have run it, go on to the next step. A step that only reads, such as new_table's preview, is never named on its own: check() names it beside the step after it.
